Single source of state for the agent team. Every agent reads this **and `PROGRESS.md`** before starting, and updates its rows + appends a PROGRESS.md worklog entry after finishing — committed and pushed the same turn. `chief-of-staff` is the runtime: each "run the company" pass moves every task as far down the lifecycle as it can and refills the pipeline via `product` when it drains.

## Scope gate (added 2026-07-26 — read before opening any task)

Passenger exists because the previous board ran 27 PRDs through this exact lifecycle, ten of them building features the strategy forbids. Every gate passed. Nobody read the strategy.

**A task may not leave `spec` unless its PRD quotes the line in `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` that authorizes it.** `product` enforces this and rejects PRDs that can't. Standing prohibitions:

- No social features of any kind — friends, following, posting, presence, profiles, avatars.
- No onboarding — the app opens straight to the map plus location permission.
- Phase 2 (proximity intelligence, **Scenic View**, **Live Events**) and Phase 3 (AI guide, shake-to-decide) are parked. Don't build toward them or leave hooks. Points is retired — replaced by the Phase 2 stamp collection system (see `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`), not a separate parked item.
- ~~**Scenic View and Live Events moved out of V1 on 2026-07-27** (decisions #19, #20).~~ **Reversed — do not read this as a prohibition.** Live Events returned to V1 on 2026-07-29 and Scenic Walk on 2026-07-30 (decisions #32, #34; `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` V1 scope). Struck rather than deleted, per convention. The salvage warning still stands: both existed in the Locali client, so watch for the *old* shapes returning through `SALVAGE.md` rather than through a PRD — the Locali events overlay was an unflagged raw feed, not the ranked subset now specced. Flagged by `product`, 2026-07-30 (PAS-10).

## Task lifecycle (state → owner)

```
backlog → spec(product) → trd(architect) → trd-review(ios-developer/developer/data-engineer + code-reviewer)
        → build(ios-developer and/or developer) → code-review(ios-code-reviewer and/or code-reviewer)
        → qa(qa) → acceptance(product) → aviran-review → done
```

**Pre-code design gate retired 2026-08-02** (Aviran, live chief-of-staff chat via `/chief` — verbatim + reasoning in `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-02 stub entry). There used to be a `design(designer)` step between `spec` and `trd`, plus `design-approval`/`design-review` sign-offs — all gone. New process: **build fast/vibe-coded first, ship, then designer redesigns after deploy.** `trd`/`build` now start straight from product's approved PRD. Designer's work moved to a non-blocking **post-ship redesign pass** — see `designer.md` — opened as its own companion task once a feature reaches `done`, never gating the original feature or any other task's build.

- **iOS vs backend split:** `build`/`code-review` fan out by surface — `ios-developer`/`ios-code-reviewer` for the Swift/SwiftUI client (`passenger-code/`), `developer`/`code-reviewer` for the Supabase backend (`passenger-brain/database/`), both pairs when a task spans both. The architect's TRD build breakdown tags each step **[iOS]**/**[Backend]**/**[Algo/Data]**.
- Marketing/research tasks: `backlog → in-progress(owner) → acceptance(product) → done`.
- Rejection loops (backward only): trd-review objection → `trd` · code-review REQUEST CHANGES → `build` · qa FAIL → `build` · acceptance REJECT → `build`. The rejecting agent writes concrete findings; the fixing agent addresses exactly those. (No more "→ `design`" fallback — there's no pre-build design step to reject back into; a design/UX finding at acceptance now goes to `build` and/or becomes input to a post-ship redesign pass.)
- `blocked-on-aviran`: strategy/scope decisions, money, external accounts (App Store, credentials), destructive ops — including every Supabase migration apply step (Aviran holds the DB credentials). Nothing else stops the loop.
- A task is `done` only after `product` ACCEPTs it against the PRD and Aviran has signed off at `aviran-review`.

Task rows: `| id | task | state | owner | updated | notes / findings / output |`

## Unowned findings & unrouted results (added 2026-08-05, L-045; widened 2026-08-06, L-050)

**Any agent may append one line here. No `T-`/`PAS-` id needed — that's the point.** Two kinds of thing belong on this list, and both are invisible anywhere else:

- **A finding** — a real defect, stale artifact, or abandoned file you found and don't own — the moment you decline to fix it, not into a worklog paragraph or a PM digest bullet.
- **A result** — work you *finished* whose `BOARD.md` row and Linear issue you are not allowed to move. "Done, `chief` routes verdicts per its lane" is a correct refusal and a dead end: `chief` is the only router and runs only when a founder asks, so a verdict handed to it after its run ends waits for the next founder request. Put it here instead, naming the row and the routing it needs.

`chief` sweeps this list each pass and converts entries into task rows, Linear updates, and issues; strike a line when it lands, naming what it became. The section exists because the previous rule ("file a `BOARD.md` row with an owner") asked non-coordinator roles to allocate ids that aren't theirs to allocate, so four separate agents correctly wrote worklog lines instead and a finished fix stayed invisible for 32 hours — and it was widened because a section titled "findings" correctly excluded three finished pieces of work on 2026-08-06, which then sat unrouted for a full day.

`- <finding or result> — suspected owner / needs routing by: <role> — found by <agent>, <date>`

- **FINDING — recurring pattern, second occurrence this session: a gate's own pass criteria turn out to be an open fail-list (silently passes a plausible-but-wrong answer) rather than a closed one.** `product` found and fixed this twice independently — once on `modal-shape-standard.md` criterion 5 during `PAS-73` (measured edges only, not height, so a visibly-broken card could honestly pass), once on `passport.md`/`places-dataset.md` req 3 during `PAS-28` (blind-read criterion only failed on a named geometry word/"don't know"/one of the six types — a plausible-but-wrong answer like "bank" passed by silence, exactly the case `qa` hit). Both times the fix was the same shape: check whether the fail list is closed (enumerates every wrong answer) or open (only catches specific known-wrong answers) before trusting a spec's pass condition. Worth a generalizable `LESSONS.md` entry — "closed vs. open fail-list" as a standing thing every future spec-writing pass should check for itself. — suspected owner: `retrospective` — found by `product`, 2026-08-07/08 (both instances)

- ~~**FINDING — the `ScrollView` + `.frame(maxHeight: 480)` pattern (`HoodSheet.swift`, and now `EventDetailModal.swift`/`PlaceDetailModal.swift` per T-079 round-3) leaves a large content-free band on short content, failing `modal-shape-standard.md` criterion 5's "no contiguous content-free band taller than ~25% of the card" for real data.** Measured live at `passenger-code` (round-3 fix commit, iPhone 17/iOS 26.5 sim, real seed data via a throwaway XCUITest + pixel scan, not source-read): `EventDetailModal` ("Rooftop set, Levinsky", full row set) has a 34.7%-of-card dead band below the Directions button; `PlaceDetailModal` ("Suzana Yemenite Kitchen", location permission declined so `RouteControls` renders `EmptyView` per its `.noOrigin` case) has a 60.2%-of-card dead band. Card-top measurement itself is correct (45.2% for both, matching `HoodSheet`'s 45% reference) — this is specifically the fixed-480pt-regardless-of-content-height behavior, not the earlier full-screen bug. Root cause: `ScrollView` with only `.frame(maxHeight:)` and no min/ideal-height wiring always claims the max under SwiftUI's sizing rules, it doesn't shrink to fit shorter content — a content-measured cap (e.g. `GeometryReader`+`PreferenceKey` to read intrinsic height, clamped to 480) would be needed to satisfy criterion 5 in the short-content case. Out of T-079 round-3's authorized scope (that task was pinned to mirroring `HoodSheet`'s existing pattern exactly, not redesigning it), and `HoodSheet` itself was never verified against a sparse Hood so may share the defect. — suspected owner: `product`/`architect` (decide whether criterion 5 needs a follow-up fix across all three files, or whether a fixed cap is accepted trade-off) — found by `ios-developer`, 2026-08-07~~ **Swept 2026-08-07 (`chief`) — filed as `PAS-77`, Backlog, no owner label yet (needs product/architect judgment call before it has one). - **RESULT — `PAS-79` still not reliably reproducible at `passenger-code 8008ff5`, and code reading suggests `PAS-78`'s fix does NOT explain/resolve it.** `qa`, checking as directed by `PAS-78`'s dispatch. Own isolated worktree/sim (see T-083 row), throwaway XCUITest driving `hourSlider.adjust(toNormalizedSliderPosition:)` with an error-corrective retry loop (read the live resolved hour off `slider.value`, i.e. `HourFormat.voiceOverValue`, and nudge the target proportionally) rather than blind retries — landed on +1/+4/+5 hours repeatedly across 6 attempts, never +3, matching the exact discrete-stop flakiness 3 prior independent sessions (`product`, `ios-code-reviewer`, earlier `qa`) already documented on this same slider. Confirmed via Apple's own `XCUIElement.h` (`XCUIAutomation.framework/Headers`) that no `.increment()`/`.decrement()` exists on `XCUIElement` for a plain slider — only `adjustToNormalizedSliderPosition:`/`adjustToPickerWheelValue:`, both documented "best effort" — so there is no more-precise public API available; stopped after 6 corrective attempts per the dispatch's "don't burn excessive time" instruction rather than retrying blind. **Code-read analysis, not a live confirmation:** the event-marker tap path (`MapScreen.handleTap`'s `EventHitTester` branch, and `EventLayer`'s own `Button` at `MapScreen.swift` ~line 334) calls `DetailRouter.openEvent(event)` directly — it never touches `chrome`/`SearchOverlay` at all, unlike the search-result-selection path `PAS-78` fixed. `DetailRouter.openEvent` (`Detail/DetailRouter.swift:46`) unconditionally sets `event`/clears `hood`/`place`, and `depth1SheetContent()`'s `.overlay` (`MapScreen.swift` ~line 553) is registered *after* the `chrome`/`SearchOverlay` `.overlay` (~line 528) in the modifier chain, so it draws on top regardless of `chrome.presented` — meaning none of `PAS-78`'s three fixes (keyboard-safe-area scoping, `chrome.dismiss()` on search selection, the narrowed `closeHood()` guard) sit on this code path. **Best current read: `PAS-79` is likely a separate, still-live bug, not resolved by `PAS-78`** — moderate confidence only, since the marker-tap repro itself was never actually reached this pass (blocked at the slider step, same as every attempt before it). Full detail: `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-07 `qa` T-083 entry. — suspected owner: whoever picks up `PAS-79` next (needs a coordinate-based slider drive or a `-uiTest`-style direct hour-offset override to get past the repro gate at all, not just another `adjustToNormalizedSliderPosition` retry) — found by `qa`, 2026-08-07

## Active phase

**Phase 1 — ship one map to real strangers in Tel Aviv.** Per `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`: nothing downstream matters until a real person reopens the app within a week, unprompted. As of 2026-07-31, this roadmap phase is itself sequenced into three **build phases** — see the section immediately below. **Build Phase 1 is the current active work.**

## V1 Build Phases (added 2026-07-31 — Aviran, live chat: "yes, replan board around the 3 phases.")

**A third, distinct meaning of "phase" in this doc — don't conflate the three:**
- **Roadmap phases** (`CLAUDE.md`, 1–5: MVP skeleton → feature buildout+signing → beta → marketing spend → new-city test). This whole build-phase structure lives entirely inside roadmap Phase 1.
- **Strategy/scope phases** (`strategy/passenger-strategy.md`, the Scope gate above): *what features exist at all* — V1 vs. parked "Phase 2" (proximity intelligence, Scenic View, Live Events) vs. parked "Phase 3" (AI local guide, shake-to-decide). **Unaffected by this section** — Live Events and Scenic Walk are confirmed in V1 scope (decisions #32/#34); this section only says *when in the build* their real data gets wired up.
- **Build phases (this section, new):** *how V1 itself gets built and shipped*, dev-sequencing only. No feature added, cut, or rescoped — same PRDs, same requirements, different order and different real-vs-fake data at each step.

**Build Phase 1 — ACTIVE. Ship the iOS app, 100% client-side, no backend.** Fake/hardcoded data baked into the app — just enough to demo interactions (saving places, the 12h time slider, browsing Hoods/places).

**Build Phase 2 — connect the real Supabase database, backfill with past/historical data, verify it works end-to-end.**

**Build Phase 3 — connect real live data.** Heat-area computation against live data, live events, TikTok/Reddit sourcing, etc.

|---|---|---|---|
| T-031 | Map — Hoods & heat area | 1 | `done`. Placeholder Hood geometry and heat-never-observed-live were carried as named gaps at acceptance — now understood as *intentional* Phase-1 fake-data scope, not defects. |

Two blocking defects, both rendered-only, both invisible to every prior gate.** **F1 (default text size, not an accessibility edge case):** `MapNavRow` draws on top of `HeatModalCard`'s readout — the Heat and Search buttons sit on the "next day" flag, which reads as "…t day". Straight deviation from the TRD's own §2.3 z5 row ("anchored a fixed distance above the nav row… never `bottom: 0`"): the card ships `ZStack(alignment: .bottom)` + `.padding(.bottom, 8)` while the nav row floats at `.padding(.bottom, 96)`. **F2 (AX5):** the readout wraps mid-token ("+12" / "h" on two lines) and the "next day" pill breaks across two lines; no `dynamicTypeSize` cap exists anywhere in the app, so AX5 is the largest supported size. **Also ran the 11 BLOCKED `qa` rows live** (disk cleared: 45Gi free, zero concurrent `xcodebuild`), on a clean detached worktree at `passenger-code d537ca5` — not the shared tree, which held T-051/T-052 WIP in `MapScreen.swift`. **Both reviewer asks now closed PASS:** C21 (`EdgeHourZoneInteractionTests` 3/3 clean, camera byte-identical across two real in-band drags) and C26 (purpose-built rotation probe: landscape drag inert, portrait control drag moves the hour). C5 PASS — `HourRepaint` avg **4ms** against a 400ms budget. C6 closed PASS by probe. 400/400 unit tests green. C22–C25 resolved by construction (no detent-sensitive code path exists). Still open, non-blocking: C14 (live VoiceOver), C20 (`mapCenterX` declared, no test uses it). Measured on the left-edge pixel column, and visually confirmed on both screenshots: `PlaceDetailModal` for "Nachum Gutman Museum" shows a title, one content row ("Things to do"), then ~1,400pt of blank white, then the Directions button. Root cause, source-confirmed: each card's `Spacer(minLength: 0)` was harmless while `.presentationDetents` bounded the height; dropping `.sheet()` removed that bound and these are **the only 2 of the 7 files with no `.frame(maxHeight:)` cap**. `HoodSheet` got a `ScrollView` + `.frame(maxHeight: 480)` **in this same commit**, with a comment stating the identical reasoning; `PlacesListOverlay`/`PassportSurface` already had it; `SearchOverlay` has a fixed fraction. Measured card tops: `PlacesListOverlay`/`PassportSurface` 37%, `HoodSheet` 45%, `SearchOverlay` 62% — all correct — vs **9%** for the two uncapped files. **This is the reviewer's own "no `ScrollView`/`maxHeight` cap" minor, re-classified: it is live, not latent.** Both `ios-code-reviewer` and `qa` tested the wrong failure mode — whether long content gets *clipped* (it doesn't) — and neither measured card height or looked at the surface's proportions, so a 91%-of-screen card with a two-thirds-empty body passed both gates. **Spec defect fixed in this same commit (L-009):** the 4 pass/fail criteria I wrote at the round-1 REJECT all measure the card's *edges* and none measures its height, so all 4 genuinely PASS on this build while the surface is visibly wrong — a new **criterion 5 (bounded height, map stays visible)** plus a scoped round-3 fix item are now in `design/phase-1/modal-shape-standard.md`. **`.medium` detent trade — my own read, differing from both reviewers: agree on the mechanism, disagree it was cost-free.** Dropping `.sheet()`/detents is right and does not need to go back to Aviran as a scope question: his verbatim ask is shape-consistency, `PAS-73`'s description explicitly scopes detent levels out, and `.medium` traces only to `PAS-48` — an agent-filed polish ticket whose own founder provenance is recorded as secondhand with no verbatim quote (L-013). A verbatim founder ask outranks that. But both gates called the cost "bounded — a glanceable-map nicety" without measuring it, and it isn't: the detent was the **only thing bounding these two cards' height**, and removing it without substituting a cap is precisely what produced the defect above. Not a scope escalation — an unfinished implementation of an accepted trade. **`PAS-75` (Search label collision): confirmed fixed**, no objection. **T-078/`PAS-60` stays held** — my round-1 routing note stands; Aviran should see one coherent screen, and this screen is not coherent yet. Verification: `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-07 `product` T-079 round-2 entry. Full detail: `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-07 `ios-code-reviewer` T-079 entry. Moving to `qa`. Prior: **`ios-developer` re-fix 2026-08-07, `passenger-code 08a09fb`.** Fixed both of `product`'s findings and pixel-verified all 7 files, not just the 3 touched last round. **F1 (Group B flush-bottom):** `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .bottom)` moved to be the outermost modifier on `SearchOverlay`/`PlacesListOverlay`/`PassportSurface` — it has to wrap the fully-laid-out card to take effect at the true extent; it was previously nested (inside the `GeometryReader` before the outer `.frame`, or on the inner `card` instead of the outer `ZStack`). **F2 (Group A never correct on iOS 26):** tested `.presentationDetents([.large])` alone first, per the spec's own "investigate whether this is sufficient" instruction — built, rendered, pixel-measured it, and it was **not** sufficient: iOS 26's Liquid Glass `.sheet()` still renders floating/all-4-corners-rounded at `.large`, confirmed by screenshot (~73pt corner radius, map visible in every corner), and confirmed against live sources (WebSearch/WebFetch, not asserted from training data) that no SwiftUI API on this toolchain forces the old page-style shape back at any detent. **Decision, made explicitly per the task's ask:** dropped `.sheet()` entirely for `EventDetailModal`/`PlaceDetailModal`/`HoodSheet`, replaced with the same custom `ZStack` scrim+card construction Group B already uses; `MapScreen` Site A and `HoodSheet`'s nested Site B now embed these views directly instead of presenting via `.sheet(isPresented:)`. Cost: `.medium` detent (and `PAS-48`'s map-glanceable-while-open state) is gone — there's no detent concept left — traded for the actually-uniform shape Aviran's original report asked for; preserving `.medium` was not achievable without leaving Group A visibly different from Group B. **`PAS-75` landed same pass** (mechanical, no design judgment needed): `SearchButton`'s "Search" VoiceOver label collided with `SearchOverlay`'s "Search" segment label — renamed to "Search and hours," every UI test that looked it up by the old label updated. **Verification: own render, all 7 files** (dedicated sim, dedicated derived-data, throwaway XCUITest deleted before commit, screenshots pixel-measured via PIL) — full width/flush bottom/top-corners-only all **PASS** for `EventDetailModal`, `PlaceDetailModal`, `HoodSheet`, `SearchOverlay` (both segments), `PlacesListOverlay`, `PassportSurface`. Own build: BUILD SUCCEEDED. 3 pre-existing test failures in the shared working tree (`ScenicWalkBundleInvariantTests`, 2× `SearchHourSegmentInteractionTests`) confirmed root-caused to a *different* concurrent session's uncommitted Hood-aliases WIP, not this diff — isolated by reconstructing a mine-only version of the 2 files both sessions touched (`SearchOverlay.swift`, `SearchHourSegmentInteractionTests.swift`) via `git show HEAD:<path>` diffing, staging/committing only that, then restoring the other session's uncommitted hunks back to the working tree untouched. Full detail: `PROGRESS.md`'s 2026-08-07 `ios-developer` T-079 entry. Ready for a fresh (not diff-scoped) `ios-code-reviewer` pass given the prior REJECT. Prior: **`product` REJECT 2026-08-07** — first-hand render, and the failure is one no earlier gate could have caught by reading source. Two concrete findings, both measured in pixels on live screenshots (`passenger-code ddbc7de`, iPhone 17/iOS 26.5), not eyeballed: **(F1) Group-B cards are not flush to the bottom edge.** `SearchOverlay` and `PlacesListOverlay` both stop **102px = 34pt** short of the screen's last row — exactly the iPhone 17 home-indicator inset — with live map rendering underneath. Confirmed twice by two independent capture paths (XCUITest attachment and `simctl io screenshot`), so it is not a screenshot artifact. Cause: `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .bottom)` is applied *inside* the `GeometryReader`, before the outer `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, maxHeight: .infinity, alignment: .bottom)`, so the card is laid out within the safe area regardless. Full width and top-corners-only both genuinely PASS — sides are flush at x=0/x=w-1. **(F2) Group A was never correct, and the spec's premise for leaving it alone is wrong on this runtime.** `modal-shape-standard.md` reasoned that a SwiftUI `.sheet()` is by default full-width/flush-bottom/top-corners-only — true through iOS 18, **false on iOS 26**, where a sheet at a non-`.large` detent renders as an inset floating card rounded on all four corners with map visible left, right and bottom. Rendered proof: `EventDetailModal` opened from `eventMarker-seed-0001`. That is precisely the shape Aviran filed the bug about, so `EventDetailModal`/`PlaceDetailModal`/`HoodSheet` need their own fix, not a "don't touch" — and "all modals across app must share this same size/shape treatment" is still unmet, because the two groups render as visibly different shapes today. **Why every prior gate passed it:** the spec stated "full-width" and "flush to the bottom edge" with no measurable pass condition, and no role rendered a Group-A surface at all — all three trusted the source-derived claim that it was already correct. Missing criteria now written into `design/phase-1/modal-shape-standard.md` (new "Pass/fail criteria" + "Correction" sections, added at acceptance per L-009) — re-fix is judged against those four measurements. **Back to `build`, not `design`** (UX problem found at acceptance is build-facing input, current lifecycle). `PAS-75`'s label fix should ride along, same files. The round-2 failure (9%, card covering the map) is gone; map renders above the card on 118/118 sampled rows for both. Three independent measurements of this commit now agree on the verdict while differing in the second digit (`ios-developer` 45.2%, `qa` ~42.8%, this pass 44.8%) — method/text-size spread, not instability. **Test-tally discrepancy resolved, not waved through.** Ran the full suite myself at clean `19861ab`: **497/498, 1 failure**, and that failure is the pre-existing tracked `ScenicWalkBundleInvariantTests.falseFlagCountAndTotal()` (`T-076`/`PAS-72`, fixture expects 24 hoods, bundle ships 44) — independently reproducing `qa`'s number exactly. | T-046 | Shared dwell/geofence "been here" detector — named by T-035, T-036, T-037, owned by none | **blocked-on-aviran** | Aviran (2 questions) → then `product` for the PRD | 2026-08-04 | **SCOPE GATE: PASSED — not parked (`product`, 2026-08-04, decision #46).** Authorized by three verbatim lines in `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`'s **V1-scope list**, not its Phase 2/3 candidate list: *""Been" places are auto-saved when you dwell 20+ minutes somewhere already in Passenger's places table"*; *"Geofence-detected plain visits (no 20-min dwell) still log too, per decision #26."*; *"**The ask is triggered by a detected visit** — when the geofence logs that someone was actually somewhere, a local notification fires…"*. The detector is not a new feature — it's the sensor three already-authorized V1 features were specified to read. **Not Phase 2's parked geofencing:** Phase 2's line is *"the app notices when you're **near** a spot you were already **heading to**"* — prospective, en-route, feeding an arrival card; this is retrospective and dwell-based, emitting a record rather than a nudge. Phase 2's own next bullet (*"Location access only turns on while you're actually en route to a suggestion — **never standing, never background**"*) is the opposite of what V1 auto-save needs, so they cannot be one mechanic. **Boundary for the builder:** emits nothing to the user beyond a `{place_id, kind, first_observed_at}` record and decision #24's local-QA notification. No arrival card, no proximity nudge, no en-route state. **CORRECTION to the row text below — it is launch-blocking.** "All three consumers ship against stand-ins" is a *build-sequencing* fact, not a launch-readiness one: without this, V1 ships with Been/Visited permanently empty, Passport permanently at zero stickers, and local-QA — half the localness pipeline — never firing. It is Build Phase 2 work **inside roadmap Phase 1**. **Two questions for Aviran, both blocking the PRD:** **(Q1)** `design/ux-flows.md` §3 proposes an **in-app priming line** ("Let Passenger notice your visits, even when it's closed?") before the Always + Notifications system prompts and labels itself *"[design call, flagged] This is a proposal, not a confirmed sequence."* Decision #8 and ux-flows Journey 1 step 3 both say "the OS system sheet, not a custom in-app screen" — but only about **cold open**. Is one in-app priming line before those two prompts acceptable, or must they arrive as bare system sheets? (Same class as T-032's Q8 edge-hint question already routed to him.) **(Q2 — the bigger one, L-013 finding):** Aviran's most specific words on this mechanic are verbatim on record and say the opposite of what we've specced. 2026-07-25, live chief-of-staff chat (`archive/2026-07-26-reset/linear-export/issues.md`, LOC-104 "Visited Places v2 — smart confirm modal + candidate ranking"): *"when the app detects the user has stayed at one location for more than 20 minutes, surface a small modal asking if they were at this place, naming the guessed place ('Were you at <place name>?'). User can answer Yes / No, or pick from a list of nearby candidate places at that location."* Today `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` §9 row 3 asserts **"no confirmation UI and no user-facing prompt exists anywhere in this feature"** as a *pass condition*. No decision row ever retired the modal; decision #26's "auto-save" wording may supersede it or may be a recorder's gloss — **[ASSUMPTION]** either way, not reasoned from. This is not cosmetic: the confirm-and-pick modal is also the answer to the ambiguity problem below, so Q2 changes the detector's whole shape. **Data/schema need (Job 1 item 7) — recommend a supporting PRD, not a bullet:** the detector needs a *monitorable place set*, and CoreLocation's classic region monitoring caps at ~20 simultaneous regions per app (**[ASSUMPTION]** on the current number — `architect` re-confirms against the SDK). Tel Aviv has 9 curated places today and is specified to grow well past 20, so "monitor every curated place" is not buildable. Either use a coarse arrival primitive and resolve the place afterwards, or maintain a rotating monitored subset — *which places, chosen how* is `data-engineer`'s question with its own quality stakes (a wrong subset means Been silently never fires for part of the city: no error, just an empty list). **Also for Aviran, not a decision:** Always requires `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription` + a background-location capability, an App Store review surface Apple scrutinises — App Store is his gate. **PRD deliberately not written this pass:** it is Build Phase 2 work while Build Phase 1 still has open gaps (T-050, T-051, T-052, T-054, T-056), and its central UX moment is Q1/Q2. Record shape and the keep-the-higher-kind update invariant are already pinned (`places-been-saved/TRD.md` §3.4) and the PRD must restate both as its own testable requirements. Prior: **Filed this run** per T-037's TRD flag (`prds/passport/TRD.md`, "left behind" section) — third TRD to name this gap without an owner (T-035 `prds/tourist-trap-flag/TRD.md`, T-036 `prds/places-been-saved/TRD.md` §7). Real CoreLocation-based detector to replace T-036's `BundledVisitSource` behind its already-built `VisitSourcing` seam; also needs an Always-location-authorization UX moment, which strategy's no-onboarding rule leaves genuinely unspecified — a product/design question, not just an engineering build. Linear `PAS-33`, backlog, unclaimed. Not launch-blocking for Phase 1 (all three consumers ship against stand-ins today); becomes real work at Phase 2/3. |

Move like a local."* asks the word to carry. **The range was fixed by data before judgement entered, at [2, 3]:** floor 2, because at 1 per-Hood Local is true on first contact and overall Local ("Local in every designated Hood") degrades into a checklist; ceiling 3, the smallest curated place count across designated Hoods, above which Local is unreachable by construction — **and that ceiling is not theoretical**: the shipped `PassportBundleInvariantTests.everyDesignatedHoodMeetsThreshold` fails at 4. Req 4's "identical polyline" rule is implemented as a **divergence test** (≥100 m contiguous run outside the fast route's 25 m corridor) because MapKit returns different point counts for the same path — a byte-equality check would never fire and the requirement would have passed by never being exercised (D8). **One risk raised that the architecture cannot solve, for Aviran/`product`: "not flagged tourist-heavy" is being used as a proxy for "worth walking through," and it is not one** — a `false` flag on, say, the old central-bus-station area would make it a recommended detour (§8 R1). **Carried unchanged from the PRD, still Aviran's:** `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`'s V1-scope navigation bullet still describes decision #32's weighted routing and now contradicts what this TRD specifies — Aviran-gated, reported not edited (`CLAUDE.md` rule 8); and req 5's disclosed gap (the scenic detour cannot survive the hand-off). Filed as **T-061**, non-blocking. **Consequence for `PAS-55`: none — the amendment stands.** `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` v3's §9 row 4 (retargeted at `borderColor`) and build step **C15** (`ios-developer`, retarget `HoodLayerFillDimTests`) were written on the [ASSUMPTION] that `PAS-49` was intended; that assumption is now confirmed, so no revert. `HoodLayer.fillColor`'s eventual deletion (post-C15) is separately unblocked by this finding. |
| T-061 | A flagged (tourist-trap) Hood shows zero heat-intensity signal on the map — `PAS-49`'s border-only render gives the flag the outline exclusively | backlog | `designer`/`product` | 2026-08-04 | **Filed by `designer` while resolving T-060/PAS-56, 2026-08-04 — a real UX gap surfaced during that investigation, not a documentation nit, so it gets its own row per `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 rather than living only inside T-060's resolution note.** Before `PAS-49` (`passenger-code c1b8bc3`), a Hood's interior fill carried heat regardless of flag state, so a viewer could read "how busy" and "tourist-heavy" independently on the same zone at a glance. After `PAS-49` removed the fill, `HoodLayer.body`'s `effectiveStroke` switch is mutually exclusive per Hood: a flagged zone's outline is `Color("Flag")`, never `borderColor`'s heat hue — so a flagged zone's stroke tells you it's flagged and, via the `busyWarning` dash pattern, whether it crossed the busy *threshold*, but not the actual heat gradation, and a flagged zone below that threshold is visually identical to one with no recent data. This is a real loss against the product's own "how busy right now, one glance" north star, specifically for the zones most worth that glance (busy + tourist-heavy is the combination the warning state exists to surface). **Not blocking anything** — `T-060`/`PAS-55` are both resolved independent of this. **Options for whoever picks this up, not yet decided:** a low-opacity heat wash reappearing behind a flagged zone's outline (reopens the density/never-color-alone questions `PAS-49` was trying to close, needs real design thought, not a reflexive revert); folding a heat cue into the neighborhood-zoom centroid label text; or accepting the trade-off explicitly as documented in `design/map-rendering-spec.md` §2 and closing this as won't-fix. Not yet a Linear issue — `chief-of-staff` to ticket if it wants one. |
